Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\impeccable

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

Tool result:
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

## Generate Report

### Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |
|---|-----------|-------|-------------|
| 1 | Accessibility | ? | [most critical a11y issue or "--"] |
| 2 | Performance | ? | |
| 3 | Responsive Design | ? | |
| 4 | Theming | ? | |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/20** | **[Rating band]** |

**Rating bands**: 18-20 Excellent (minor polish), 14-17 Good (address weak dimensions), 10-13 Acceptable (significant work needed), 6-9 Poor (major overhaul), 0-5 Critical (fundamental issues)

### Anti-Patterns Verdict
**Start here.** Pass/fail: Does this look AI-generated? List specific tells. Be brutally honest.

### Executive Summary
- Audit Health Score: **??/20** ([rating band])
- Total issues found (count by severity: P0/P1/P2/P3)
- Top 3-5 critical issues
- Recommended next steps

### Detailed Findings by Severity

Tag every issue with **P0-P3 severity**:
- **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion — fix immediately
- **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation — fix before release
- **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists — fix in next pass
- **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact — fix if time permits

For each issue, document:
- **[P?] Issue name**
- **Location**: Component, file, line
- **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Anti-Pattern
- **Impact**: How it affects users
- **WCAG/Standard**: Which standard it violates (if applicable)
- **Recommendation**: How to fix it
- **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

### Patterns & Systemic Issues

Identify recurring problems that indicate systemic gaps rather than one-off mistakes:
- "Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens"
- "Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience"

### Positive Findings

Note what's working well — good practices to maintain and replicate.

## Recommended Actions

List recommended commands in priority order (P0 first, then P1, then P2):

1. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context from audit findings)
2. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context)

**Rules**: Only recommend commands from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset. Map findings to the most appropriate command. End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended.

After presenting the summary, tell the user:

> You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.
>
> Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see your score improve.

**IMPORTANT**: Be thorough but actionable. Too many P3 issues creates noise. Focus on what actually matters.

**NEVER**:
- Report issues without explaining impact (why does this matter?)
- Provide generic recommendations (be specific and actionable)
- Skip positive findings (celebrate what works)
- Forget to prioritize (everything can't be P0)
- Report false positives without verification

Remember: You're a technical quality auditor. Document systematically, prioritize ruthlessly, cite specific code locations, and provide clear paths to improvement.
> **Additional context needed**: what the interface is trying to accomplish.

### Gather Assessments

Launch two independent assessments. **Neither may see the other's output** — this isolation is what makes the combined score honest. Running both in one head silently anchors them to each other; do not shortcut it for cost, speed, or context-size reasons.

Delegate each assessment to a separate sub-agent (Claude Code's `Agent` tool, Codex's subagent spawning, etc.). Each returns structured findings as text. Do NOT output findings to the user yet.

Fall back to sequential in-head work only if the environment genuinely cannot spawn sub-agents.

**Tab isolation**: When browser automation is available, each assessment MUST create its own new tab. Never reuse an existing tab, even if one is already open at the correct URL. This prevents the two assessments from interfering with each other's page state.

#### Assessment A: LLM Design Review

Read the relevant source files (HTML, CSS, JS/TS) and, if browser automation is available, visually inspect the live page. **Create a new tab** for this; do not reuse existing tabs. After navigation, label the tab by setting the document title:
```javascript
document.title = '[LLM] ' + document.title;
```
Think like a design director. Evaluate:

**AI Slop Detection (CRITICAL)**: Does this look like every other AI-generated interface? Review against ALL **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Check for AI color palette, gradient text, dark glows, glassmorphism, hero metric layouts, identical card grids, generic fonts, and all other tells. **The test**: If someone said "AI made this," would you believe them immediately?

**Holistic Design Review**: visual hierarchy (eye flow, primary action clarity), information architecture (structure, grouping, cognitive load), emotional resonance (does it match brand and audience?), discoverability (are interactive elements obvious?), composition (balance, whitespace, rhythm), typography (hierarchy, readability, font choices), color (purposeful use, cohesion, accessibility), states & edge cases (empty, loading, error, success), microcopy (clarity, tone, helpfulness).

**Cognitive Load** (consult [cognitive-load](cognitive-load.md)):
- Run the 8-item cognitive load checklist. Report failure count: 0-1 = low (good), 2-3 = moderate, 4+ = critical.
- Count visible options at each decision point. If >4, flag it.
- Check for progressive disclosure: is complexity revealed only when needed?

**Emotional Journey**:
- What emotion does this interface evoke? Is that intentional?
- **Peak-end rule**: Is the most intense moment positive? Does the experience end well?
- **Emotional valleys**: Check for anxiety spikes at high-stakes moments (payment, delete, commit). Are there design interventions (progress indicators, reassurance copy, undo options)?

**Nielsen's Heuristics** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)):
Score each of the 10 heuristics 0-4. This scoring will be presented in the report.

Return structured findings covering: AI slop verdict, heuristic scores, cognitive load assessment, what's working (2-3 items), priority issues (3-5 with what/why/fix), minor observations, and provocative questions.

#### Assessment B: Automated Detection

Run the bundled deterministic detector, which flags 25 specific patterns (AI slop tells + general design quality).

**CLI scan**:
```bash
npx impeccable --json [--fast] [target]
```

- Pass HTML/JSX/TSX/Vue/Svelte files or directories as `[target]` (anything with markup). Do not pass CSS-only files.
- For URLs, skip the CLI scan (it requires Puppeteer). Use browser visualization instead.
- For large directories (200+ scannable files), use `--fast` (regex-only, skips jsdom)
- For 500+ files, narrow scope or ask the user
- Exit code 0 = clean, 2 = findings

**Browser visualization** — **required** when browser automation tools are available AND the target is a viewable page. The `[Human]` overlay tab is the user-facing deliverable; the critique is incomplete without it. Skip only if the target is not a viewable page (CSS-only file, non-browser target).

The overlay is a **visual aid for the user**. It highlights issues directly in their browser. Do NOT scroll through the page to screenshot overlays. Instead, read the console output to get the results programmatically.

1. **Start the live detection server**:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live &
   ```
   Note the port printed to stdout (auto-assigned). Use `--port=PORT` to fix it.
2. **Create a new tab** and navigate to the page (use dev server URL for local files, or direct URL). Do not reuse existing tabs.
3. **Label the tab** via `javascript_tool` so the user can distinguish it:
   ```javascript
   document.title = '[Human] ' + document.title;
   ```
4. **Scroll to top** to ensure the page is scrolled to the very top before injection
5. **Inject** via `javascript_tool` (replace PORT with the port from step 1):
   ```javascript
   const s = document.createElement('script'); s.src = 'http://localhost:PORT/detect.js'; document.head.appendChild(s);
   ```
6. Wait 2-3 seconds for the detector to render overlays
7. **Read results from console** using `read_console_messages` with pattern `impeccable`. The detector logs all findings with the `[impeccable]` prefix. Do NOT scroll through the page to take screenshots of the overlays.
8. **Cleanup**: Stop the live server when done:
   ```bash
   npx impeccable live stop
   ```

For multi-view targets, inject on 3-5 representative pages. If injection fails, continue with CLI results only.

Return: CLI findings (JSON), browser console findings (if applicable), and any false positives noted.

### Generate Combined Critique Report

Synthesize both assessments into a single report. Do NOT simply concatenate. Weave the findings together, noting where the LLM review and detector agree, where the detector caught issues the LLM missed, and where detector findings are false positives.

Structure your feedback as a design director would:

#### Design Health Score
> *Consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md)*

Present the Nielsen's 10 heuristics scores as a table:

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key Issue |
|---|-----------|-------|-----------|
| 1 | Visibility of System Status | ? | [specific finding or "n/a" if solid] |
| 2 | Match System / Real World | ? | |
| 3 | User Control and Freedom | ? | |
| 4 | Consistency and Standards | ? | |
| 5 | Error Prevention | ? | |
| 6 | Recognition Rather Than Recall | ? | |
| 7 | Flexibility and Efficiency | ? | |
| 8 | Aesthetic and Minimalist Design | ? | |
| 9 | Error Recovery | ? | |
| 10 | Help and Documentation | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/40** | **[Rating band]** |

Be honest with scores. A 4 means genuinely excellent. Most real interfaces score 20-32.

#### Anti-Patterns Verdict

**Start here.** Does this look AI-generated?

**LLM assessment**: Your own evaluation of AI slop tells. Cover overall aesthetic feel, layout sameness, generic composition, missed opportunities for personality.

**Deterministic scan**: Summarize what the automated detector found, with counts and file locations. Note any additional issues the detector caught that you missed, and flag any false positives.

**Visual overlays** (if browser was used): Tell the user that overlays are now visible in the **[Human]** tab in their browser, highlighting the detected issues. Summarize what the console output reported.

#### Overall Impression
A brief gut reaction: what works, what doesn't, and the single biggest opportunity.

#### What's Working
Highlight 2-3 things done well. Be specific about why they work.

#### Priority Issues
The 3-5 most impactful design problems, ordered by importance.

For each issue, tag with **P0-P3 severity** (consult [heuristics-scoring](heuristics-scoring.md) for severity definitions):
- **[P?] What**: Name the problem clearly
- **Why it matters**: How this hurts users or undermines goals
- **Fix**: What to do about it (be concrete)
- **Suggested command**: Which command could address this (from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

#### Persona Red Flags
> *Consult [personas](personas.md)*

Auto-select 2-3 personas most relevant to this interface type (use the selection table in the reference). If `AGENTS.md` contains a `## Design Context` section from `impeccable teach`, also generate 1-2 project-specific personas from the audience/brand info.

For each selected persona, walk through the primary user action and list specific red flags found:

**Alex (Power User)**: No keyboard shortcuts detected. Form requires 8 clicks for primary action. Forced modal onboarding. High abandonment risk.

**Jordan (First-Timer)**: Icon-only nav in sidebar. Technical jargon in error messages ("404 Not Found"). No visible help. Will abandon at step 2.

Be specific. Name the exact elements and interactions that fail each persona. Don't write generic persona descriptions; write what broke for them.

#### Minor Observations
Quick notes on smaller issues worth addressing.

#### Questions to Consider
Provocative questions that might unlock better solutions:
- "What if the primary action were more prominent?"
- "Does this need to feel this complex?"
- "What would a confident version of this look like?"

**Remember**:
- Be direct. Vague feedback wastes everyone's time.
- Be specific. "The submit button," not "some elements."
- Say what's wrong AND why it matters to users.
- Give concrete suggestions, not just "consider exploring..."
- Prioritize ruthlessly. If everything is important, nothing is.
- Don't soften criticism. Developers need honest feedback to ship great design.

### Ask the User

**After presenting findings**, use targeted questions based on what was actually found. STOP and use Codex's structured user-input/question tool when available; if unavailable, ask directly in chat to clarify what you cannot infer. These answers will shape the action plan.

Ask questions along these lines (adapt to the specific findings; do NOT ask generic questions):

1. **Priority direction**: Based on the issues found, ask which category matters most to the user right now. For example: "I found problems with visual hierarchy, color usage, and information overload. Which area should we tackle first?" Offer the top 2-3 issue categories as options.

2. **Design intent**: If the critique found a tonal mismatch, ask whether it was intentional. For example: "The interface feels clinical and corporate. Is that the intended tone, or should it feel warmer/bolder/more playful?" Offer 2-3 tonal directions as options based on what would fix the issues found.

3. **Scope**: Ask how much the user wants to take on. For example: "I found N issues. Want to address everything, or focus on the top 3?" Offer scope options like "Top 3 only", "All issues", "Critical issues only".

4. **Constraints** (optional; only ask if relevant): If the findings touch many areas, ask if anything is off-limits. For example: "Should any sections stay as-is?" This prevents the plan from touching things the user considers done.

**Rules for questions**:
- Every question must reference specific findings from the report. Never ask generic "who is your audience?" questions.
- Keep it to 2-4 questions maximum. Respect the user's time.
- Offer concrete options, not open-ended prompts.
- If findings are straightforward (e.g., only 1-2 clear issues), skip questions and go directly to Recommended Actions.

### Recommended Actions

**After receiving the user's answers**, present a prioritized action summary reflecting the user's priorities and scope from Ask the User.

#### Action Summary

List recommended commands in priority order, based on the user's answers:

1. **`$command-name`**: Brief description of what to fix (specific context from critique findings)
2. **`$command-name`**: Brief description (specific context)
...

**Rules for recommendations**:
- Only recommend commands from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset
- Order by the user's stated priorities first, then by impact
- Each item's description should carry enough context that the command knows what to focus on
- Map each Priority Issue to the appropriate command
- Skip commands that would address zero issues
- If the user chose a limited scope, only include items within that scope
- If the user marked areas as off-limits, exclude commands that would touch those areas
- End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended

After presenting the summary, tell the user:

> You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.
>
> Re-run `$impeccable critique` after fixes to see your score improve.

# Product

## Scope

App-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.

A rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.

The repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.

## Platform

web

## Users

**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.

**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.

**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.

No .claude/launch.json found. Create C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\.claude\launch.json with this format:
{
  "version": "0.0.1",
  "configurations": [
    {
      "name": "<unique-name>",
      "runtimeExecutable": "<command>",
      "runtimeArgs": ["<args>"],
      "port": <port>
    }
  ]
}
Set "runtimeExecutable" to the command (e.g. "npm"), "runtimeArgs" to the arguments (e.g. ["run", "dev"]), and "port" to the server port. An optional "url" (http/https) opens the preview there instead of http://localhost:<port>. A localhost "url" must be just the server's origin — no path or query, matching the entry's port — for example "https://localhost:8443" or "http://app.localhost:3000"; to show a specific page, navigate after the preview opens. Non-localhost URLs may carry paths and are subject to the user's permission and the organization's browsing policy. A configuration with "url" and no command attaches to an already-running server. Only include servers you actually need to preview. Then call preview_start with the server name.

3	# Product
4	
5	<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->
6	
7	## Scope
8	
9	App-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.
10	
11	A rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.
12	
13	The repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.
14	
15	## Platform
16	
17	web
18	
19	## Users
20	
21	**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.
22	
23	**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.
24	
25	**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.
26	
27	## Product Purpose
28	
29	LSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.
30	
31	For a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.
32	
33	For Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.
34	
35	Success is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.
36	
37	## Positioning
38	
39	**A real coach is in it.**
40	
41	The claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.
42	
43	## Operating Context
44	
45	The platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.
46	
47	- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.
48	- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).
49	- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.
50	- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.
51	- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.
52	
53	## Capabilities and Constraints
54	
55	Confirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the bootcamps, and an admin and teacher workspace.
56	
57	Technical constraints:
58	
59	- React, TypeScript, Vite, Tailwind, shadcn/ui and Radix primitives, React Router, TanStack Query, Supabase.
60	- One motion library (`framer-motion`). One primary icon library (`lucide-react`). New work composes existing primitives before introducing a new dependency.
61	- Deployment is not a side effect of pushing to `main`. Production at lsatprep.study requires an explicit publish step.
62	- Database migrations in `supabase/migrations/` are an archive, not an applied ledger. Production schema changes go through their own path, and UI must not assume a table exists because a migration file does.
63	
64	Terminology is not defined here. It is defined per bounded context and mapped by `CONTEXT-MAP.md`. A word can carry different weight in different contexts, and collapsing them has already caused real bugs (a **flag** is not a **wrong answer**; **Set** and **Position** were separated for this reason).
65	
66	## Brand Commitments
67	
68	- **Name:** LSAT U, operating under Aspiring Attorneys. Production domain lsatprep.study.
69	- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect reads.** Joshua does not type them. A stray em-dash reads as machine-written and costs trust. This binds computed copy, utility copy, and authored copy alike.
70	- **Voice: undecided, deliberately.** The previous record carried a "coach voice, not industry voice" principle and two named voice registers (R1 procedural, R2 metaphor-led). Both were removed on 2026-09-05 by Joshua's decision, on the grounds that they were not stated clearly enough to be usable. They are being redone. Until a replacement is written, no work should cite the old voice rules, and no work should invent a voice rule to fill the gap. Route student-facing copy to Joshua.
71	
72	## Evidence on Hand
73	
74	Real:
75	
76	- A live production deployment at lsatprep.study with real enrolled students.
77	- Authored LSAT curriculum: bootcamp modules, guided drill banks, RC passages with per-question explanations, and practice test content.
78	- Real student work in the database: attempts, sessions, flags, wrong answers, assignments.
79	- A real named instructor with a real coaching practice behind the product.
80	
81	Absent, and never to be fabricated:
82	
83	- No testimonials, case studies, press coverage, score-improvement statistics, or named customer references exist in this repo. Do not write them into any surface.
84	- No pricing, licensing, or availability claims are established here.
85	- No third-party or copyright framing anywhere in student-facing copy.
86	
87	## Product Principles
88	
89	1. **Truthfulness over performance.** Every number, badge, streak, status, and progress claim traces to real stored data. An honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops. This is the one principle carried forward unchanged from the previous record, and it is the hardest of the five.
90	
91	2. **Compute the next step; do not present a grid.** A student arriving at a surface should be told what to do, not shown a menu and left to choose. A card grid is what a product offers when it does not know the student.
92	
93	3. **Attempt before explanation.** The student commits to an answer before the product explains anything. Passive reading does not build the skill. Reveal copy addresses what the student actually chose, not a generic key.
94	
95	4. **A measurement is never a gate.** Drills and checks measure after teaching. A miss is framed as the next thing to work on, not a grade or a failure. Nothing in the product rewards attendance rather than work.
96	
97	5. **The coach is not simulated.** Where the product speaks for Joshua, it is because Joshua said it. Where he has not, the product does not fill the silence with something that sounds like him. This is the operational form of the positioning claim.
98	
99	## Accessibility & Inclusion
100	
101	**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.
102	
103	- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
104	- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
105	- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
106	- Interactive targets meet the 44x44px minimum, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text.
107	- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
108	- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.
109	
110	=== DESIGN ===
111	---

244	
245	# Design System: LSAT Academy
246	
247	## Overview
248	
249	**Creative North Star: "LSAT Academy"**
250	
251	One institution with two registers, and the seam between them is deliberate.
252	
253	**The Hall** is the door. Unauthenticated, dark, and material: near-black ground, warm marble text, a bronze that carries the light, and a serif that behaves like carved lettering. Its own source calls it a museum nocturne, carved marble filmed in darkness, read by modern instruments. Its job is to make a prospective student feel they are entering somewhere, not signing up for something. It is the only place in the product where atmosphere outranks throughput.
254	
255	**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom's white they collapse. The Hall's bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.
256	
257	What they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.
258	
259	Inside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room's paint.
260	
261	**Key Characteristics:**
262	- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.
263	- Colour means something or it is absent.
264	- Flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows.
265	- Precise and quiet controls that stay out of the reader's way.
266	- Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are.
267	
268	## Colors
269	
270	Two grounds, one shared vocabulary of meaning.
271	
272	### Primary
273	
274	- **Workroom Ink** (`#121212`): the Workroom's near-black. It is the text colour, the fill of every primary button, and the colour of the focus ring. The Workroom has no accent, so ink carries emphasis by itself.
275	- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall's warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall's night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.
276	
277	### Secondary
278	
279	- **Hall Cyan** (`#8ad8e2`): the Hall's cool instrument note against the bronze, and the colour of its focus outlines. Rare by design; it marks precision where bronze marks warmth.
280	- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.
281	
282	### Tertiary
283	
284	Individual rooms carry their own palettes behind a wrapper class and are documented in their own surface briefs, not here. The wrappers in use are `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk`, and the eight Activity Centre worlds.
285	
286	### Neutral
287	
288	- **Workroom Page** (`#fcfcfc`) and **Card** (`#ffffff`): the page ground and the surface that sits on it. One step of separation, no more.
289	- **Workroom Raised** (`#f7f7f7`) and **Fill** (`#f2f2f2`): the two tonal steps available for layering and for resting fills.
290	- **Workroom Ink Muted** (`#737373`): secondary text. Measures 4.62:1 on the page, which clears the floor with almost nothing to spare. Do not lighten it.
291	- **Workroom Hairline** (`#e0e0e0`) and **Field** (`#e6e6e6`): the borders that do the work shadows used to.
292	- **Hall Marble** (`#eae5da`), with secondary (`#a6a093`) and muted (`#6e695e`) steps: warm off-white text on the night ground.
293	
294	### State (semantic only, never decorative)
295	
296	- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student's attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.
297	- **Warn** (`#f59f0a`): a bonus, a stretch, a non-blocking notice.
298	- **Success** (`#21c45d`), **Warning** (`#f59f0a`), **Destructive** (`#dc2828`): system status, not student performance.
299	
300	### Named Rules
301	
302	**The Ground Decides Rule.** A warm colour is legible on the Hall's night and illegible on the Workroom's page. Never carry a colour across the seam on the strength of liking it; measure it against the ground it will land on first.
303	
304	**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.
305	
306	**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.
307	
308	## Typography
309	
310	**Workroom Body:** Lexend (with Outfit, then the system stack)
311	**Hall Display:** Fraunces (with Iowan Old Style, Times New Roman, serif)
312	**Hall Inscription:** Cinzel (with Trajan Pro, serif)
313	**Mono:** IBM Plex Mono
314	**Question Lock:** Outfit, held deliberately for LSAT question surfaces
315	
316	**Character:** The Workroom is one humanist sans doing everything, sized and weighted rather than swapped. The Hall is a serif institution: an optical serif for its headings and an inscriptional face for its few carved lines. The split is the whole typographic idea. Inside, type gets out of the way; at the door, type is the architecture.
317	
318	### Hierarchy
319	
320	- **Title** (600, 24px, line-height 1, tracking -0.025em): card and section headings in the Workroom.
321	- **Body** (400, 14px rising to 16px at 640px and above, line-height 1.6): everything a student reads in the Workroom chrome.
322	- **Question** (400, 16px, line-height 1.6, Outfit): LSAT stimulus and answer choices. Font-locked to Outfit even though the rest of the app moved to Lexend, so question text does not shift when the shell's face changes.
323	- **Label** (500, 14px): button and control labels.
324	- **Hall Display** (380, fluid, tracking -0.015em, Fraunces): Hall headings. The unusually light 380 weight is the point; it reads as carved rather than printed.
325	- **Hall Inscription** (500, tracking 0.05em, Cinzel): the Hall's few short carved lines. Never body copy.
326	- **Hall Mono** (400, 13px, tracking 0.04em, IBM Plex Mono): the Hall's instrument labels.
327	
328	### Named Rules
329	
330	**The One Face Rule.** The Workroom uses a single family. Hierarchy is built from size, weight and space. A second family inside the Workroom is a defect unless it is the Outfit question lock.
331	
332	**The Question Never Moves Rule.** LSAT question text is font-locked. A change to the shell's typeface must not reflow a passage a student is mid-way through reading.
333	
334	**The Serif Stays At The Door Rule.** Fraunces and Cinzel belong to the Hall. A serif appearing in the Workroom chrome means the seam has leaked.
335	
336	## Layout
337	
338	The Workroom is a centred single column with generous side gutters. The container centres by default, carries 32px of horizontal padding, and stops widening at 1400px.
339	
340	Breakpoints are the framework defaults up to 1280px, with the largest step pulled in to 1400px. Body type steps from 14px to 16px at 640px, and form controls are forced to 16px below 768px so that iOS does not zoom the page when a field takes focus. Named breakpoints `phone` (up to 600px), `tablet` (601 to 900px) and `desktop` (901px and up) exist for bootcamp surfaces.
341	
342	Spacing runs on a 4px base. The steps in real use are 4, 8, 16, 24 and 32. Card padding is 24px on all sides; button padding is 8px vertical and 16px horizontal.
343	
344	Reading measure matters more here than in most products. Passage and stimulus text is held to a comfortable line length rather than allowed to run the full container width.
345	
346	### Named Rules
347	
348	**The Reading Measure Rule.** Any surface carrying an LSAT passage, stimulus, or explanation constrains its own line length. The container's maximum is a ceiling for layout, never a licence for prose.
349	
350	## Elevation & Depth
351	
352	**Flat, with hairline separation.** Surfaces sit on the page rather than above it. A card is distinguished from its ground by a one-pixel border and, where more is needed, by one step of background tone. There are no drop shadows in the Workroom.
353	
354	This is a decision, not a description. The codebase currently ships five shadow tokens tuned for a dark ground, from `0 1px 3px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` up to `0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.7)`, and applies the smallest of them to every card. Because the app ships light by default, those shadows land at 40 percent black under a white card. The light theme never redefined them, and the one glow token is white, so it is invisible on white. The tokens are retired by this record and the code has not caught up yet.
355	
356	The Hall is the exception and keeps its own depth: fixed ambience gradients, a grain overlay, and an inset highlight on its call to action. That depth is atmospheric rather than structural, and it does not transfer.
357	
358	### Named Rules
359	
360	**The Flat Rule.** Depth in the Workroom comes from a hairline or a tonal step. A `box-shadow` on a Workroom surface is a defect.
361	
362	**The Two-Step Rule.** Page, then card, then at most one raised step. A surface that needs a fourth level of layering is a surface that needs restructuring.
363	
364	## Shapes
365	
366	Corners are gently rounded and consistent: 8px for cards and containers, 6px for buttons and inputs, 4px for the smallest chips and insets, and a full pill only for genuine badges. The scale derives from one 0.5rem radius token, so a change to the base moves the whole system together.
367	
368	The Hall uses a near-square 2px radius instead. Its edges are cut, not softened, which is what separates a carved surface from a card.
369	
370	Borders are one pixel. There is no second border weight in the Workroom.
371	
372	### Named Rules
373	
374	**The Hairline Rule.** One pixel, always. A thicker border used as a coloured accent stripe on the leading edge of a surface is banned, including when faked with an inset shadow. Active state is carried by background tone.
375	
376	## Components
377	
378	### Buttons
379	
380	- **Character:** precise and quiet. A button is a plain rectangle with a label. It darkens slightly on hover and does nothing else. It does not lift, does not cast a shadow, and does not scale on press.
381	- **Shape:** gently rounded (6px).
382	- **Primary:** ink fill (`#121212`), page-coloured label, 8px by 16px padding.
383	- **Outline:** page background, one-pixel field border, ink label.
384	- **Ghost:** no fill at rest, a tonal fill on hover.
385	- **Sizes:** default 44px tall, small 40px, large 48px, icon 44 by 44. Every size clears the 44px touch minimum by being that height, never by relying on wrapped text or padding accidents.
386	- **Focus:** a solid two-pixel ink outline, offset two pixels from the edge. This is the only focus treatment in the Workroom.
387	- **Hall CTA:** 60px tall, near-square corners, transparent fill over a bronze hairline, with a bronze sheen that sweeps across on hover and a cyan outline on focus.
388	
389	### Cards / Containers
390	
391	- **Corner:** 8px.
392	- **Background:** card white on the page ground.
393	- **Border:** one-pixel hairline. This is what separates the card, not a shadow.
394	- **Shadow:** none.
395	- **Padding:** 24px, with header, content and footer sharing that inset.
396	
397	### Inputs / Fields
398	
399	- **Style:** page background, one-pixel field border, 6px corners, 44px minimum height, 8px by 12px padding.
400	- **Focus:** the same two-pixel ink outline as buttons, offset two pixels. Do not express focus as a border colour change alone; a border shift is too quiet to serve as the only indicator.
401	- **Mobile:** 16px text below 768px, so focusing a field never zooms the viewport.
402	
403	### Navigation
404	
405	Neutral chrome throughout. The sidebar runs one tonal step off the page ground with a hairline edge, and the active item is marked by background tone rather than by a coloured rule on its leading edge.
406	
407	### Signature Component: the scoped world
408	
409	The product's distinctive pattern is not a control, it is a container. A room commits fully to its own palette, typefaces and atmosphere behind a single wrapper class, and nothing inside it leaks out. Scene layers are `aria-hidden` furniture painted beneath the content, so the accessible tree is identical whether the room is painted or bare. A new world is added by writing a new wrapper and its prefixed tokens, never by adding hex to a shared component.
410	
411	## Do's and Don'ts
412	
413	### Do:
414	
415	- **Do** resolve every colour from a token, and treat a token that resolves to nothing as a defect. Verify a new token renders before documenting it.
416	- **Do** give every interactive element a visible focus indicator: a solid two-pixel ink outline offset two pixels in the Workroom, cyan in the Hall.
417	- **Do** check contrast against the ground the colour will actually land on, in the theme that actually ships, in a browser. The theme file has read as correct while the page rendered a light card on a near-black ground.
418	- **Do** separate surfaces with a one-pixel hairline and one tonal step.
419	- **Do** keep colour semantic: correct, incorrect, warning, danger. Nothing else earns it.
420	- **Do** scope a new room's identity behind its own wrapper class with prefixed tokens, the way the bootcamps and the eight Activity worlds already do.
421	- **Do** hold LSAT passage and stimulus text to a readable line length, and leave it on the Outfit question lock.
422	- **Do** end every world's stylesheet with a `prefers-reduced-motion` block that stops continuous motion rather than speeding it up.
423	
424	### Don't:
425	
426	- **Don't** add a `box-shadow` to a Workroom surface. Depth is hairline and tone.
427	- **Don't** carry a Hall colour into the Workroom. Bronze reads 8.58:1 on the Hall's night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page.
428	- **Don't** introduce a warm or metallic accent into the Workroom to make it feel premium. Premium here is depth, hierarchy and type.
429	- **Don't** remove the browser's focus outline without shipping a replacement in the same rule. `outline-none` with no working ring is worse than leaving the default alone.
430	- **Don't** animate the focus indicator. A `transition-all` on a control means the ring fades in, which delays the one signal a keyboard user depends on.
431	- **Don't** use a border thicker than one pixel as a coloured accent stripe, including when faked with an inset shadow.
432	- **Don't** put a second typeface into the Workroom chrome, and don't let a serif cross in from the Hall.
433	- **Don't** animate layout properties. Animate `opacity`, `transform` and `color`. No bounce, no elastic, no lift on hover.
434	- **Don't** write a hex value into a component. If a room needs its own colour, promote it to named tokens behind that room's wrapper.
435

# Guided Drills & Explanations

The practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).

## Language

**Question Explanation**:
The question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions — this is the headline gap (Phase 2).
_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that's only one half), answer key, rationale.

**Drill Instruction Card**:
The drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill — teaches the pattern the drill targets ("these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence..."). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its "Info" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).
_Avoid_: info card (that's LSAT Lab's name), intro, launch card, explanation.

**Question Type**:
The LSAT task a question asks — *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank's raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 "Unknown") and need a canonical set (~25 types).
_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that's Patterns/Process), question kind.

**Question Feature**:
A finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type — *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.
_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that's a separate field).

**Reasoning Type**:
The underlying logical structure — *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.
_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.

**Drill Signature** (proposed):
The accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.
_Avoid_: deriving a drill's targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).

**Guided Drill**:
A curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).
_Avoid_: drill (too generic — could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.

**Answer Navigator** (the "bubbles"):
The strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**
per question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the
current question's number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by
`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals "bubbles"; the code calls them
ovals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.
One oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters
because "bubble" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer
(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a
verdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.
_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar's
`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),
and using "bubble" for an A-E answer choice.

**Oval State**:
What one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,
and they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a
qid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and
**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there
is no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had
to say whether an answer was right.
_Avoid_: "completed" or "done" (answered does not mean finished or correct),
"selected" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).

**Drill Launch**:
Everything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of
drill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere
else, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch
names a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given
and the position in the queue live in the Run Record.
_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in
the code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.

**Drill Run Spec**:
A Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the
filter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.
Built by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by
`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.
The Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is
told. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.
_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second
time), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.

**Run Record**:
The durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:
the ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already
