Lifted out of the hero because the hero\n * remounts on every section flip, and re-querying profiles to redraw the same\n * name would be a database round trip per toggle.\n */\nfunction useGreetingName() {\n  const { user } = useAuth();\n  const [profileDisplayName, setProfileDisplayName] = React.useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  React.useEffect(() => {\n    if (!user) return;\n    let cancelled = false;\n    (async () => {\n      const { data } = await supabase\n        .from(\"profiles\")\n        .select(\"display_name\")\n        .eq(\"class_id\", user.id)\n        .maybeSingle();\n      if (!cancelled) setProfileDisplayName(data?.display_name ?? null);\n    })();\n    return () => {\n      cancelled = true;\n    };\n  }, [user]);\n\n  return React.useMemo(() => {\n    const raw =\n      profileDisplayName ||\n      (user?.user_metadata?.display_name as string | undefined) ||\n      user?.email?.split(\"@\")[0] ||\n      \"\";\n    const name = raw.split(/[\\s._-]/)[0].replace(/[^a-zA-Z]/g, \"\");\n    return name ? name.charAt(0).toUpperCase() + name.slice(1).toLowerCase() : \"\";\n  }, [profileDisplayName, user]);\n}\n\nfunction MainhubHero({\n  section,\n  firstName,\n  drillReturnTo,\n  focus,\n  onOpenBuilder,\n  onStartSmartRc,\n}: {\n  section: DrillSection;\n  firstName: string;\n  drillReturnTo: string;\n  focus: TodayFocus;\n  onOpenBuilder: () => void;\n  onStartSmartRc: () => void;\n}) {\n  const navigate = useNavigate();\n\n  /* Same shape as Classroom Home's dateline (\"TUESDAY · SEPTEMBER 8 · 2026\"),\n     so the two mounts one click apart do not print the date two ways. */\n  const todayLabel = React.useMemo(() => {\n    const d = new Date();\n    const weekday = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: \"long\" });\n    const monthDay = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: \"long\", day: \"numeric\" });\n    return `${weekday} · ${monthDay} · ${d.getFullYear()}`;\n  }, []);\n\n  const startSmartDrill = React.useCallback(() => {\n    navigate(\"/drill\", { state: { mode: \"adaptive\", returnTo: drillReturnTo } });\n  }, [drillReturnTo, navigate]);\n\n  const startFallback = section === \"rc\" ? onStartSmartRc : startSmartDrill;\n  const fallbackLabel = section === \"rc\" ? \"Start One Passage\" : \"Start Smart Drill\";\n\n  /* The hero leads with the computed next step when there is one. Assigned\n     work is section-agnostic (Joshua assigns it, the student does not choose\n     a bank first), so it leads on RC too. Smart Drill / One Passage become\n     the quiet second option; with nothing assigned they are the first. */\n  const primary = !focus.loading && !focus.hasError ? focus.primary : null;\n  const NextIcon = primary?.Icon;\n\n  return (\n    /* Surface, scrim and grid live in .drill-observatory-hero / -hero-grid: the\n       horizon image needs image-set() and a two-layer scrim pseudo-element,\n       neither of which belongs in a className. */\n    <Card\n      data-section={section}\n      className=\"drill-observatory-hero w-full border-0 bg-transparent p-0 shadow-none\"\n    >\n      <div className=\"drill-observatory-hero-grid\">\n        <div className=\"drill-observatory-greeting flex flex-col justify-end\">\n          <div className=\"flex flex-col gap-[9px]\">\n            <p className=\"drill-observatory-label drill-observatory-ink-60\">\n              {todayLabel}\n            </p>\n            {/* Size, face and the italic on the name come from\n                .drill-observatory-greeting h1 — the CSS rule outranks utilities\n                here, so putting them in className would be dead code. */}\n            <h1>\n              {firstName ? (\n                <>\n                  Welcome back, <em>{firstName}</em>\n                </>\n              ) : (\n                \"Welcome back\"\n              )}\n            </h1>\n\n            {focus.loading ? (\n              /* Same height as the next-up block so the CTA does not jump when\n                 Today resolves. */\n              <div className=\"drill-observatory-next animate-pulse\" aria-label=\"Loading today\">\n                <div className=\"drill-observatory-skeleton-bar h-2.5 w-28\" />\n                <div className=\"drill-observatory-skeleton-bar mt-2 h-5 w-64 max-w-full\" />\n                <div className=\"drill-observatory-skeleton-bar mt-1 h-3 w-20\" />\n              </div>\n            ) : primary ? (\n              <div className=\"drill-observatory-next\">\n                <p className=\"drill-observatory-label drill-observatory-label-sm drill-observatory-ink-60 flex items-center gap-2\">\n                  {NextIcon && <NextIcon className=\"h-3.5 w-3.5\" strokeWidth={1.8} aria-hidden />}\n                  Next up · {primary.sourceLine}\n                </p>\n                <p className=\"drill-observatory-next-title\">{primary.title}</p>\n                {(primary.status || primary.actionCue) && (\n                  <p className=\"drill-observatory-label drill-observatory-label-sm drill-observatory-ink-55\">\n                    {[primary.status, primary.actionCue].filter(Boolean).join(\" · \")}\n                  </p>\n                )}\n              </div>\n            ) : (\n              <p className=\"drill-observatory-ink-70 text-sm leading-[1.55]\">\n                {section === \"rc\"\n                  ? \"Read one passage now, untimed, with the answers after it. Or build a set of passages around exactly what you want to read.\"\n                  : \"Start an adaptive set that follows your weakest question types, or build a drill around exactly what you want to practice.\"}\n              </p>\n            )}\n          </div>\n\n          <div className=\"drill-observatory-cta-row\">\n            {primary ? (\n              <>\n                <Button\n                  onClick={() => void focus.open(primary)}\n                  disabled={!primary.route && !primary.onOpen}\n                  className=\"drill-observatory-cta w-fit\"\n                >\n                  {actionText(primary)}\n                  <ArrowRight className=\"h-3.5 w-3.5\" strokeWidth={2} aria-hidden />\n                </Button>\n                <button\n                  type=\"button\"\n                  onClick={startFallback}\n                  className=\"drill-observatory-quiet-link\"\n                >\n                  {section === \"rc\" ? \"Read one passage instead\" : \"Start a Smart Drill instead\"}\n                </button>\n              </>\n            ) : (\n              <Button onClick={startFallback} className=\"drill-observatory-cta w-fit\">\n                {/* RC's \"smart\" is one untimed passage, not the adaptive engine\n                    LR's button opens. Same word, two promises — so it says what\n                    it starts. */}\n                {fallbackLabel}\n                <ArrowRight className=\"h-3.5 w-3.5\" strokeWidth={2} aria-hidden />\n              </Button>\n            )}\n          </div>\n        </div>\n\n        <div className=\"drill-observatory-console w-full\">\n          <div className=\"flex h-full flex-col justify-between gap-6\">\n            <div className=\"space-y-3\">\n              <h2 className=\"drill-observatory-ink-full text-[clamp(1.35rem,3cqi,2rem)] font-semibold leading-tight tracking-[-0.025em]\">\n                {section === \"rc\" ? \"Build the reading set you need.\" : \"Build the drill you need.\"}\n              </h2>\n              <p className=\"drill-observatory-ink-70 max-w-[48ch] text-sm leading-[1.6]\">\n                {section === \"rc\"\n                  ? \"Choose PrepTests, topics, formats, frameworks, and difficulty independently. Hand-pick the exact passages, or let the set fill itself.\"\n                  : \"Choose PrepTests, question types, reasoning, features, and levels independently. Preview the exact set before you start.\"}\n              </p>\n            </div>\n            <Button\n              type=\"button\"\n              onClick={onOpenBuilder}\n              className=\"drill-observatory-console-start\"\n            >\n              {section === \"rc\" ? \"Set up an RC drill\" : \"Set up an LR drill\"}\n              <ArrowRight className=\"h-4 w-4\" strokeWidth={2} aria-hidden />\n            </Button>\n          </div>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </Card>\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Launcher stage — hosts the Build a Set panel. className=\"drill-observatory-label drill-observatory-ink-60\">\n              Loading questions…\n            </p>\n          </div>\n        )}\n\n        {!isLoading && error && (\n          <div className=\"flex flex-col items-center gap-4 py-14 text-center\" role=\"alert\">\n            <p className=\"drill-observatory-ink-85 text-sm font-medium\">{error}</p>\n            <Button\n              type=\"button\"\n              onClick={() => window.location.reload()}\n              className=\"drill-observatory-console-start !w-auto px-6\"\n            >\n              Reload the question )}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Drill hub stage. URL first, then what this browser\n     remembers, then LR — see `src/lib/drillSection.ts`. Reading it from the\n     URL rather than from state is what makes a refresh, a Back press, and a\n     shared link all land in the same place. */\n  const sectionParam = searchParams.get(DRILL_SECTION_PARAM);\n  const section = React.useMemo(() => resolveDrillSection(sectionParam), [sectionParam]);\n\n  /* A bare URL states no section, so it resolves from what this browser\n     remembers — and memory MOVES. Land on /classroom/drill in LR, throw the\n     switch to RC, then press Back: the bare entry re-resolves against the\n     now-RC memory and Back appears to do nothing.\n\n     So the first thing the hub does is write the resolved section into the\n     URL, replacing the entry rather than pushing one. The three cards each carry\n          26-30px of internal padding, so the gap between them stays smaller than\n          their padding by design — they read as three objects on one field, not\n          as one split surface. */}\n      <div className=\"drill-observatory-canvas relative mx-auto flex w-full max-w-[71.25rem] flex-col gap-5\">\n        {/* The address row rides above the hero rather than inside it: the\n            route label row is decorative and aria-hidden, and the hero's two\n            columns are already the page's two primary actions. Side by side they read\n              as what they are: the two utilities of this band. */}\n          <div className=\"drill-observatory-tools-aside\">\n            <QuestionFinder returnTo={drillReturnTo} tone=\"dark\" bank={section} />\n            <SectionToggle section={section} onChange={changeSection} />\n          </div>\n        </div>\n\n        {/* The hero stays mounted across section changes (its content swaps in\n            place so the LR/RC switch never shows an empty frame), and leaves\n            the page entirely while a stage is open. (\n          <section\n            className=\"drill-observatory-analytics\"\n            aria-label=\"Analytics workspace\"\n            data-tour=\"mainhub-analytics\"\n          >\n            <AnalyticsStage\n              tab={analyticsTab}\n              section={section}\n              onTabChange={setAnalyticsTab}\n            />\n          </section>\n        ) : (\n          /* A launcher swaps out ONLY the practice bay. (\n                <LauncherStage\n                  kind={stage}\n                  section={section}\n                  drillReturnTo={drillReturnTo}\n                  onCancel={closeStage}\n                />\n              ) : (\n                <PracticeInsightStage\n                  section={section}\n                  onOpenAnalytics={openAnalytics}\n                  onOpenLauncher={openLauncher}\n                  canOpenAnalytics={canOpenAnalytics}\n                  canOpenJournal={canOpenJournal}\n                />\n              )}\n            </section>\n\n            {/* The hero owns the primary item only while it is on the page.\n                With a stage open the hero is gone, so Today says the next step\n                itself again. */}\n            <section className=\"drill-observatory-today-bay\">\n              <TodayFocusPanel\n                drillReturnTo={drillReturnTo}\n                focus={focus}\n                heroOwnsPrimary={stage === \"default\"}\n              />\n            </section>\n          </section>\n        )}\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu\\.impeccable\\surfaces\\drill-hub.md", "content": "# Surface brief: the drill hub\n\nRoute: `/classroom/drill` (also `/practice`, which redirects). Code: `src/pages/AcademyMainhub.tsx`, `src/components/mainhub/`, the `.drill-observatory-*` block of `src/components/classroom/classroom.css`, `src/components/drill/SectionToggle.tsx`. Wrapper: `.classroom-root-drill` and `.drill-observatory-root`.\n\nWritten 2026-09-08 from the impeccable audit and critique, after Joshua's two decisions: Today's assigned item leads the hero, and this hub is a sanctioned scoped world with its own record. This file is that record. DESIGN.md's Workroom rules do not apply inside the wrapper; the rules below do.\n\n## What the page is for\n\nA paying client logs in to do what Joshua assigned. The page tells them that first, in the hero, with one button. Everything else on the page (adaptive drill, the builder, the question jump, insights) is the fallback for a student with nothing assigned, or the second thing after the assignment. Product principle 2 (compute the next step, do not present a grid) is the whole layout brief.\n\nOrder of the page, top to bottom: utilities (jump, search, LR/RC), the hero (greeting, Next up, one CTA, one quiet alternative, the builder console), the practice card (insights, Analytics, Journal), Today (the queue behind the hero's item).\n\n## The ground\n\nThe stage is cream (`--classroom-drill-page`). The cards on it are near-black (`--observatory-surface-card` `#08080b`). Two of the three cards carry a photograph (the horizon behind the hero, the eclipse behind Today), and those two photographs are the only decoration on the page. No accent colour. No gradients. No grid textures.\n\nControls that sit on the stage itself (the jump row, the section switch) are inked for the stage: `--observatory-stage-*`. Controls on a card are inked ivory: the `--observatory-ink*` ladder. The 2026-09-08 audit found the jump row inked for the cards while sitting on the stage, at 1.07:1. Tone is decided by what a control sits on, never by what is near it.\n\n## Ink and contrast\n\nWCAG AA is a defect gate here as everywhere. Measured against `#08080b`, the ivory ladder passes for text from `--observatory-ink-50` (4.9:1) upward. The steps below it (0.38, 0.42, 0.45, 0.48) were removed on 2026-09-08; they were carrying labels at 3.2:1 to 4.2:1. Nothing that carries text goes below `ink-50`. Hairlines, rings and skeleton bars use their own tokens and may be fainter.\n\nOn the stage: `--observatory-stage-ink` 12.9:1, `--observatory-stage-muted` 5.3:1 on `--observatory-stage-well`.\n\nOver the photographs, text sits on a scrim (`::before` on the hero and on Today's inner surface). The scrim is mandatory; the photographs have bright crops.\n\n## Type\n\nThree families, on purpose, one job each:\n\n- Fraunces, weight 400, `opsz` 60: the greeting only (`h1`). It is the one serif line on the page. Insight values and Today titles use it at 20 to 22px as the page's second display voice. Nothing else.\n- JetBrains Mono, 10 to 11px, tracked 0.16 to 0.18em, uppercase: micro-labels and chips. Never a sentence.\n- Outfit: everything else. Body 13.5 to 14px, the Next up title 19px semibold, the console heading fluid.\n\n## Depth\n\nHairline rings (`0 0 0 1px`) separate surfaces. Shadows are quiet: `0 12px 32px` at 12 to 16 percent, not the 60px at 25 to 35 percent the page shipped with. Glass (`backdrop-filter`) appears only where text sits over a photograph: the hero console and Today's inner surfaces. A glass panel on the flat black practice card is a defect.\n\n## Controls\n\n- 44px minimum by height, met by the control: the CTA (46px), the console button (46px), the section switch options (44px), selects (44px), secondary links and insight actions (44px). No pseudo-element hit areas.\n- Focus: a 2px solid ivory outline (`--observatory-focus`), offset 3px, on every pill control. Full-bleed rows inside a clipped container use the inset variant (`.drill-observatory-focus-inset`). On the stage, a 2px `--observatory-stage-ink` outline. No control removes an outline without one of these.\n- Hover: a brightness or colour change. Rails and tiles may lift 2px; the primary CTA never moves.\n- Motion: `--observatory-motion` (160ms) for state; the section switch's indicator is a spring. Transform and opacity only. The RC flip's height snaps. `prefers-reduced-motion` stops everything.\n\n## Doors\n\nOne door to the builder: the hero console. The practice card does not repeat it. An insight may offer a contextual action (open analytics, open the journal, drill a weak type) because it is a response to real data, not a menu item.\n\nWhile a stage (builder, analytics) is open, the hero is not rendered. The stage carries its own title and Cancel.\n\n## Copy\n\nNo em-dashes anywhere a student can read, including placeholders. Buttons say what they do (\"Start\", \"Resume drill\", \"Set up an LR drill\"). \"Smart Drill\" is the product's name for the adaptive drill and stays, with the sentence before it saying what it does. The date reads the same as Classroom Home: `Tuesday · September 8 · 2026`.\n\n## Known debt (not fixed on 2026-09-08)\n\n- 206 `!important` declarations in the observatory block. They exist to override shadcn's Card and Button. The right fix is unstyled primitives for this surface.\n- The hub ignores the app's dark toggle: the stage is cream either way. Acceptable while the stage is documented as cream here.\n- `.drill-observatory-practice-tile` and `PracticeTile` are still exported for the RC flip and are no longer used on the LR default stage.\n"}

## assistant

Everything that doesn't need a signed-in session is done and passing. The one remaining step is visual verification, and the local dev server has no session: it landed on the sign-in page. I can't enter credentials, so I need you to sign in on the localhost tab in the in-app browser (it's open at http://localhost:8080/auth). Once you're in, say "signed in" and I'll run the contrast, target-size, focus and mobile measurements against the new build and screenshot the hero.

**Done so far, all verified by typecheck and tests (41 passing across hub, Today, switch and picker):**

- **Today leads the hero.** One shared hook computes the next step; the hero shows "Next up · Assigned by Joshua", the title, status, and one button ("Start", "Resume drill"). Smart Drill becomes a quiet underlined alternative. With nothing assigned, the old hero returns. Today's panel becomes "Also today" with the queue only, so nothing is said twice.
- **One door to the builder.** The "Build a Set" tile and the rotating carousel are gone. The practice card is now "Your practice": every real insight listed at once, then Analytics and Journal under a hairline.
- **Hero leaves while a stage is open**, so the launcher's own title is first on screen.
- **Contrast.** Jump row now inked for the cream stage. Ink ladder floored at 0.50 (4.9:1); the four failing steps deleted from tokens and every usage. Inactive LR/RC and select placeholders raised to 5.3:1.
- **Focus.** One 2px ivory outline rule for every pill control, inset variant for full-bleed rows, dark outline on the switch.
- **Targets.** Switch options, selects, finder trigger, secondary links and insight actions at 44px. Menu button 44px.
- **Phone.** Drill stage gets the 72px top inset so the fixed menu button no longer covers content. Today rails get `min-w-0`, which fixes the 432px overflow.
- **Slop trims.** Shadows cut from 60px/35% to 32px/16%. Flip no longer animates height. Em-dashes removed from the placeholder and the insight formatter. Date now matches Home with the year. Literal colours in JSX replaced by tokens.
- **Surface brief** written at [.impeccable/surfaces/drill-hub.md](projects/lsatu/.impeccable/surfaces/drill-hub.md) so the dark room is sanctioned with its own contrast floor and glass rule.

**Not done:** nothing is committed or published. The 206 `!important` overrides and the theme-toggle gap are recorded as debt in the brief, not fixed.

/* ── Question finder ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
   One control that turns something a student can say about a question into
   that question opened. Mounted on Classroom Home and in the Drill landing
   workspace, so it carries its own token block rather than inheriting either
   host's palette: the trigger takes the host's tone, the panel floats above
   the page and always looks like itself.

   Follows the Classroom's rules, because Home is where it is seen most:
   every hairline is a box-shadow ring rather than a border, so a hover state
   cannot shift anything by a pixel; every animation is transform/opacity only,
   so nothing here can reflow while it moves. */

.qf {
  --qf-ink: #332f2c;
  --qf-ink-mid: #5c5751;
  --qf-body: #6b6660;
  --qf-soft: #8a857f;
  --qf-muted: #a7a29f;

  --qf-surface: #ffffff;
  --qf-surface-warm: #fffdf9;
  --qf-row-hover: #fdf7ef;
  --qf-sunk: #f8f4ee;

  --qf-accent: #ff8906;
  --qf-accent-text: #e5730a;
  --qf-gold: #c5820a;

  --qf-hair-06: rgba(68, 64, 61, 0.06);
  --qf-hair-08: rgba(68, 64, 61, 0.08);
  --qf-hair-12: rgba(68, 64, 61, 0.12);

  --qf-ui: "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --qf-mono: "JetBrains Mono", ui-monospace, monospace;
  --qf-ease: cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);
}

## assistant

Yes, your first push went through and Lovable published it. I verified the new build live on lsatprep.study and pushed one follow-up fix that needs another publish.

**Confirmed live on production**

- The hero now leads with "Next up · Assigned by Joshua", your Conditional Logic cheat sheet, a Start button, and "Start a Smart Drill instead" as the quiet alternative.
- The practice card shows three real insights as a list (Top priority, Accuracy trend, Practice volume), no carousel.
- Today reads "Also today" and lists only the queue behind the hero's item.
- Keyboard focus on the Start button draws the ivory ring.
- On a 375px phone: no horizontal overflow, the menu button no longer covers content, the rails no longer clip.
- Every interactive control measures at least 44px. No em-dashes anywhere on the page. Heading order is h1, h2, h2, h2.

**One defect survived, now fixed and pushed as `1d1d351`**

The Test / Section / Question labels were still ivory on cream. The tone switch worked, but the row's field rules read finder tokens that only exist inside the finder's own wrapper, so they fell through to the observatory's ivory. The jump row now declares those tokens itself in both tones. Finder tests pass (56/56).

Publish that one in Lovable and the jump row will read dark on cream. Say "published" and I'll re-measure it.

## assistant

No, it is not supposed to be invisible. That's the Test / Section / Question jump row, and it is the exact defect I just pushed the fix for in commit `1d1d351`. Your screenshot is the build before that commit.

Why it happened: the row's labels and dropdowns are styled by the question finder's rules, which read colour tokens that only exist inside the finder's own wrapper. On the drill hub the row sits outside that wrapper, so the tokens resolved to nothing and the text inherited the page's ivory ink, which is invisible on the cream stage. My first commit switched the row to the light tone, but the light tone only set the row's own tokens, not the names the rules actually read. The second commit declares them.

Publish `1d1d351` in Lovable and the row will render dark text on cream. Say "published" and I'll measure it on production to confirm.

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\impeccable

Designs and iterates production-grade frontend interfaces. Real working code, committed design choices, exceptional craft.

## Setup (non-optional)

Before any design work or file edits, pass these gates. Skipping them produces generic output that ignores the project.

| Gate | Required check | If fail |
|---|---|---|
| Context | The PRODUCT.md / DESIGN.md loader result is known from `node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs`. | Run the loader before continuing. |
| Product | PRODUCT.md exists and is not empty or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars). | Run `$impeccable teach`, refresh context, then resume. Never synthesize PRODUCT.md from the user's original prompt alone. |
| Command | The matching command reference is loaded when a sub-command is used. | Load the reference before continuing. |
| Craft | `$impeccable craft` has a user-confirmed shape brief for this task. `teach` / PRODUCT.md never counts as shape. | Run `$impeccable shape` and wait for explicit brief confirmation. |
| Image | Required visual probes / mocks are generated or skipped with a reason. | Resolve the image-generation gate in `shape.md` or `craft.md` before code. |
| Mutation | All active gates above pass. | Do not edit project files yet. |

Codex-style agents must state this before editing files:

```text
IMPECCABLE_PREFLIGHT: context=pass product=pass command_reference=pass shape=pass|not_required image_gate=pass|skipped:<reason> mutation=open
```

For `$impeccable craft`, `shape=pass` is only valid after a separate user response approving the shape design brief, or when the user provided an already-confirmed brief in the request. Do not mark `shape=pass` after writing PRODUCT.md, summarizing assumptions, or drafting an unconfirmed brief yourself.

Other harnesses should follow the same checklist when they can expose this state.

### 1. Context gathering

Two files, case-insensitive. The loader looks at the project root by default and falls back to `.agents/context/` and `docs/` if the root is clean. Override with `IMPECCABLE_CONTEXT_DIR=path/to/dir` (absolute or relative to cwd).

- **PRODUCT.md** — required. Users, brand, tone, anti-references, strategic principles.
- **DESIGN.md** — optional, strongly recommended. Colors, typography, elevation, components.

Load both in one call:

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/load-context.mjs
```

Consume the full JSON output. Never pipe through `head`, `tail`, `grep`, or `jq`. The output's `contextDir` field tells you where the files were resolved from.

If the output is already in this session's conversation history, don't re-run. Exceptions requiring a fresh load: you just ran `$impeccable teach` or `$impeccable document` (they rewrite the files), or the user manually edited one.

`$impeccable live` already warms context via `live.mjs` — if you've run `live.mjs`, don't also run `load-context.mjs` this session.

If PRODUCT.md is missing, empty, or placeholder (`[TODO]` markers, <200 chars): run `$impeccable teach`, then resume the user's original task with the fresh context. If the original task was `$impeccable craft`, resume into `$impeccable shape` before any implementation work.

If DESIGN.md is missing: nudge once per session (*"Run `$impeccable document` for more on-brand output"*), then proceed.

### 2. Register

Every design task is **brand** (marketing, landing, campaign, long-form content, portfolio — design IS the product) or **product** (app UI, admin, dashboard, tool — design SERVES the product).

Identify before designing. Priority: (1) cue in the task itself ("landing page" vs "dashboard"); (2) the surface in focus (the page, file, or route being worked on); (3) `register` field in PRODUCT.md. First match wins.

If PRODUCT.md lacks the `register` field (legacy), infer it once from its "Users" and "Product Purpose" sections, then cache the inferred value for the session. Suggest the user run `$impeccable teach` to add the field explicitly.

Load the matching reference: [reference/brand.md](reference/brand.md) or [reference/product.md](reference/product.md). The shared design laws below apply to both.

## Shared design laws

Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.

### Color

- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.

### Theme

Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."

Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.

"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.

### Typography

- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.

### Layout

- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.

### Motion

- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.

### Absolute bans

Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.

- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.

### Copy

- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.

### The AI slop test

If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.

**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

## Commands

| Command | Category | Description | Reference |
|---|---|---|---|
| `craft [feature]` | Build | Shape, then build a feature end-to-end | [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) |
| `shape [feature]` | Build | Plan UX/UI before writing code | [reference/shape.md](reference/shape.md) |
| `teach` | Build | Set up PRODUCT.md and DESIGN.md context | [reference/teach.md](reference/teach.md) |
| `document` | Build | Generate DESIGN.md from existing project code | [reference/document.md](reference/document.md) |
| `extract [target]` | Build | Pull reusable tokens and components into design system | [reference/extract.md](reference/extract.md) |
| `critique [target]` | Evaluate | UX design review with heuristic scoring | [reference/critique.md](reference/critique.md) |
| `audit [target]` | Evaluate | Technical quality checks (a11y, perf, responsive) | [reference/audit.md](reference/audit.md) |
| `polish [target]` | Refine | Final quality pass before shipping | [reference/polish.md](reference/polish.md) |
| `bolder [target]` | Refine | Amplify safe or bland designs | [reference/bolder.md](reference/bolder.md) |
| `quieter [target]` | Refine | Tone down aggressive or overstimulating designs | [reference/quieter.md](reference/quieter.md) |
| `distill [target]` | Refine | Strip to essence, remove complexity | [reference/distill.md](reference/distill.md) |
| `harden [target]` | Refine | Production-ready: errors, i18n, edge cases | [reference/harden.md](reference/harden.md) |
| `onboard [target]` | Refine | Design first-run flows, empty states, activation | [reference/onboard.md](reference/onboard.md) |
| `animate [target]` | Enhance | Add purposeful animations and motion | [reference/animate.md](reference/animate.md) |
| `colorize [target]` | Enhance | Add strategic color to monochromatic UIs | [reference/colorize.md](reference/colorize.md) |
| `typeset [target]` | Enhance | Improve typography hierarchy and fonts | [reference/typeset.md](reference/typeset.md) |
| `layout [target]` | Enhance | Fix spacing, rhythm, and visual hierarchy | [reference/layout.md](reference/layout.md) |
| `delight [target]` | Enhance | Add personality and memorable touches | [reference/delight.md](reference/delight.md) |
| `overdrive [target]` | Enhance | Push past conventional limits | [reference/overdrive.md](reference/overdrive.md) |
| `clarify [target]` | Fix | Improve UX copy, labels, and error messages | [reference/clarify.md](reference/clarify.md) |
| `adapt [target]` | Fix | Adapt for different devices and screen sizes | [reference/adapt.md](reference/adapt.md) |
| `optimize [target]` | Fix | Diagnose and fix UI performance | [reference/optimize.md](reference/optimize.md) |
| `live` | Iterate | Visual variant mode: pick elements in the browser, generate alternatives | [reference/live.md](reference/live.md) |

Plus two management commands — `pin <command>` and `unpin <command>`, detailed below.

### Routing rules

1. **No argument** — render the table above as the user-facing command menu, grouped by category. Ask what they'd like to do.
2. **First word matches a command** — load its reference file and follow its instructions. Everything after the command name is the target.
3. **First word doesn't match** — general design invocation. Apply the setup steps, shared design laws, and the loaded register reference, using the full argument as context.

Setup (context gathering, register) is already loaded by then; sub-commands don't re-invoke `$impeccable`.

If the first word is `craft`, setup still runs first, but [reference/craft.md](reference/craft.md) owns the rest of the flow. If setup invokes `teach` as a blocker, finish teach, refresh context, then resume the original command and target.

## Pin / Unpin

**Pin** creates a standalone shortcut so `$<command>` invokes `$impeccable <command>` directly. **Unpin** removes it. The script writes to every harness directory present in the project.

```bash
node .agents/skills/impeccable/scripts/pin.mjs <pin|unpin> <command>
```

Valid `<command>` is any command from the table above. Report the script's result concisely — confirm the new shortcut on success, relay stderr verbatim on error.

Classroom-wide behaviour, not the hub's; the fix is a top bar that scrolls with the page or a bottom placement. `$impeccable adapt`, classroom shell.\n\n**[P2] Glass on three surfaces.** Console, Today primary, Today rails. Recorded in the brief.\n\n**[P3] The hub ignores the dark toggle.** Recorded in the brief as acceptable while the stage is documented cream.\n\n## Design Health Score\n\n| # | Heuristic | Run 1 | Run 2 | Note |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Visibility of system status | 3 | 4 | Next up with status on one line; loading skeleton holds the CTA's place |\n| 2 | Match system / real world | 3 | 3 | \"Smart Drill\" still an adjective; kept as the product's name |\n| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | 3 | Unchanged |\n| 4 | Consistency and standards | 1 | 3 | Jump row tone fixed, date matches Home, one focus treatment; three families remain by brief |\n| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | 3 | Launcher error now has a reload action |\n| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | 4 | All insights visible at once, nothing hidden behind a timer |\n| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 3 | 3 | Shortcut still works, no longer advertised |\n| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist design | 2 | 3 | One door, one pill, hero leaves during a stage |\n| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | 3 | Reload button in the launcher |\n| 10 | Help and documentation | 2 | 2 | Nothing explains the adaptive drill beyond one sentence |\n| **Total** | **25/40** | **31/40** | |\n\n### Anti-patterns verdict\n\nPass, with one honest caveat. The page no longer has the tells that made it fail: no carousel, no hero-metric block, no side-stripes, no three-equal-pills column, no orphaned chip word, shadows at 12 to 16 percent. What remains is the lane itself: a serif greeting over a planet photograph with mono micro-labels. It is well executed and now documented as this room's identity, but a design director would still name the family. Changing that is a rebrand of the hub, not a fix, and it is not recommended.\n\n### Overall impression\n\nThe page now does the one thing PRODUCT.md asks for: it says what to do. Pass.\n- **Jordan (first session, phone):** menu button clear at the top; the hero's two options are one button and one link, not two buttons. Pass, with the scrolled-menu-button caveat.\n\n### Minor observations\n\n- The Today rails truncate long titles (\"Conditional Logic: the Cheat Sh…\"); the full title is in the hero, so acceptable.\n- \"Start a Smart Drill instead\" underline is faint (0.35 alpha) on the photo. Legible, but a 0.5 alpha would read more clearly as a link.\n- The console paragraph on a 375px phone runs five lines. A shorter phone variant would tighten the hero.\n\n## Recommended actions\n\n1. `$impeccable adapt` (classroom shell): the fixed menu button on phones.\n2. `$impeccable polish`: date label at 1024px, link underline alpha, phone console copy.\n3. `$impeccable quieter`: decide whether the Today rails need blur.\n"}

  const navigate = useNavigate();

  /* Same shape as Classroom Home's dateline ("TUESDAY · SEPTEMBER 8 · 2026"),
     so the two mounts one click apart do not print the date two ways. */
  const todayLabel = React.useMemo(() => {
    const d = new Date();
    const weekday = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { weekday: "long" });
    const monthDay = d.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { month: "long", day: "numeric" });
    return `${weekday} · ${monthDay} · ${d.getFullYear()}`;
  }, []);

  const startSmartDrill = React.useCallback(() => {
    navigate("/drill", { state: { mode: "adaptive", returnTo: drillReturnTo } });
  }, [drillReturnTo, navigate]);

                  {actionText(primary)}
                  <ArrowRight className="h-3.5 w-3.5" strokeWidth={2} aria-hidden />

                  onClick={startFallback}

---- SAFEPUSH
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Safe push to origin/main when Lovable may have committed in parallel.
# Fetches, fast-forwards or merges (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes.

set -e
cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

# Typecheck gate: tsc does not run during `vite build` (esbuild strips types), so a
# type error (e.g. a missing import that crashes at runtime) would otherwise ship to
# main and auto-deploy. Block the push on any compile error. Set SKIP_TYPECHECK=1 to
# bypass for an emergency push.
if [ "${SKIP_TYPECHECK:-0}" != "1" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Typechecking (npm run typecheck)..."
  if ! npm run --silent typecheck; then
    echo "[safe-push] Typecheck FAILED. Fix the errors above or push with SKIP_TYPECHECK=1." >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  echo "[safe-push] Typecheck passed."
fi

git fetch origin main --quiet

LOCAL=$(git rev-parse HEAD)
REMOTE=$(git rev-parse origin/main)
BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main)

if [ "$LOCAL" = "$REMOTE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] Already up to date with origin/main."
elif [ "$REMOTE" = "$BASE" ]; then
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has no new commits. Pushing."
else
  echo "[safe-push] origin/main has diverged (Lovable likely committed). Merging."
  git merge origin/main --no-edit
fi

git push origin main
echo "[safe-push] Pushed $(git log -1 --format='%h %s')"

Contrast scan\n\n```js\n(() => {\n  const L = ([r,g,b]) => { const f = c => { c /= 255; return c <= 0.03928 ? c/12.92 : ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4; }; return 0.2126*f(r)+0.7152*f(g)+0.0722*f(b); };\n  const parse = s => { const m = (s.match(/[\\d.]+/g)||[]).map(Number); return { rgb: m.slice(0,3), a: m.length > 3 ? m[3] : 1 }; };\n  const over = (top, a, under) => top.map((c,i) => Math.round(c*a + under[i]*(1-a)));\n  const bgOf = el => { let n = el, stack = []; while (n && n.nodeType === 1) { const { rgb, a } = parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor); if (a > 0) stack.push({ rgb, a }); if (a >= 1) break; n = n.parentElement; } let out = [255,255,255]; for (let i = stack.length-1; i >= 0; i--) out = over(stack[i].rgb, stack[i].a, out); return out; };\n  const ratio = (a, b) => { const x = L(a), y = L(b); return (Math.max(x,y)+0.05)/(Math.min(x,y)+0.05); };\n  const root = document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root') || document.body;\n  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);\n  const fails = [], seen = new Set(); let count = 0, node;\n  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {\n    const t = node.textContent.trim(); if (!t) continue;\n    const el = node.parentElement; if (seen.has(el)) continue; seen.add(el);\n    const cs = getComputedStyle(el); if (cs.visibility === 'hidden' || cs.display === 'none') continue;\n    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); if (r.width === 0 || r.height === 0) continue;\n    const fg = parse(cs.color); const fgRgb = fg.a < 1 ? over(fg.rgb, fg.a, bgOf(el)) : fg.rgb;\n    const bg = bgOf(el); const c = ratio(fgRgb, bg);\n    const size = parseFloat(cs.fontSize), bold = parseInt(cs.fontWeight) >= 700;\n    const large = size >= 24 || (size >= 18.66 && bold);\n    const need = large ? 3 : 4.5; count++;\n    if (c < need) fails.push({ text: t.slice(0,40), ratio: +c.toFixed(2), need, color: cs.color, bg: `rgb(${bg})`, cls: el.className.toString().slice(0,60) });\n  }\n  return { checked: count, fails };\n})()\n```\n\nCaveat: text over a photograph reports the scrim colour, not the photo. For the hero and the Today card, also zoom the screenshot and confirm by eye that the lightest part of the photo under any text still reads. If unsure, darken the scrim.\n\n### 1b. Target size scan\n\n```js\n(() => {\n  const els = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root a, .drill-observatory-root button, .drill-observatory-root [role=\"button\"], .drill-observatory-root input, .drill-observatory-root select, .drill-observatory-root [tabindex=\"0\"]')];\n  const small = els.filter(e => { const r = e.getBoundingClientRect(); return r.width > 0 && r.height > 0 && (r.height < 44 || r.width < 44) && !(r.width >= 44 && r.height >= 44); })\n    .map(e => ({ text: (e.innerText || e.getAttribute('aria-label') || '').trim().slice(0,30), w: Math.round(e.getBoundingClientRect().width), h: Math.round(e.getBoundingClientRect().height), cls: e.className.toString().slice(0,50) }));\n  return { total: els.length, under44: small };\n})()\n```\nText links inside a sentence (not this page's case) are exempt. Everything else here must be 44 by height.\n\n### 1c. Focus ring scan\n\n```js\n(() => {\n  const els = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root a, .drill-observatory-root button, .drill-observatory-root input, .drill-observatory-root select, .drill-observatory-root [tabindex=\"0\"]')];\n  const bad = [];\n  for (const e of els) { e.focus({ preventScroll: true }); const cs = getComputedStyle(e); const ok = (cs.outlineStyle !== 'none' && parseFloat(cs.outlineWidth) >= 2) || cs.boxShadow.includes('inset'); if (!ok && document.activeElement === e) bad.push({ text: (e.innerText||e.getAttribute('aria-label')||'').trim().slice(0,30), outline: cs.outline, shadow: cs.boxShadow.slice(0,50) }); }\n  document.activeElement.blur();\n  return { total: els.length, noRing: bad };\n})()\n```\nSome browsers only apply `:focus-visible` on keyboard focus, so if this reports many, confirm manually: press Tab from the top and screenshot each stop.\n\n### 1d. Headings, em-dashes, overflow, width\n\n```js\n(() => {\n  const hs = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root h1, .drill-observatory-root h2, .drill-observatory-root h3, .drill-observatory-root h4')].map(h => h.tagName + ': ' + h.innerText.trim().slice(0,40));\n  const em = (document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root')?.innerText.match(/\\u2014/g) || []).length;\n  const wide = [...document.querySelectorAll('.drill-observatory-root *')].filter(e => e.getBoundingClientRect().right > window.innerWidth + 1).map(e => e.className.toString().slice(0,50)).slice(0,10);\n  return { viewport: window.innerWidth, docWidth: document.documentElement.scrollWidth, headings: hs, emDashes: em, pastRightEdge: wide };\n})()\n```\n`docWidth` must equal `viewport`. `pastRightEdge` must be empty. Headings must read h1 then h2s, no skips.\n\n### 1e. Glass and important counts (run locally, not in the browser)\n\n```bash\ngrep -c 'backdrop-filter' src/components/classroom/classroom.css; grep -c '!important' src/components/classroom/classroom.css\n```\n\n### 1f. Viewports to measure at\n\nResize the in-app browser to each and run 1a to 1d at each: 375 wide (phone), 390, 768 (tablet), 1024, 1280, 1536. Take one full-page screenshot at 375 and at 1280 for the report. Scroll to the bottom at 375 and screenshot again (this catches the menu button problem).\n\n### 1g. Baseline\n\nBefore any change: run 1a to 1e at 375 and 1280 on the current production build (f791016). Save the outputs in the report under \"Baseline\". Expected: contrast fails 0, under44 empty, headings h1/h2/h2/h2, em-dashes 0, backdrop-filter 8 lines (4 surfaces), important 200.\n\n---\n\n## Part 2. What each lost point is, and which WP fixes it\n\n| Score row | Now | Target | Work packages |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Audit 2 Performance | 3 | 5 | WP-A (glass down to one surface), WP-B (photos, fonts, Lighthouse) |\n| Audit 3 Responsive | 3 | 5 | WP-C (menu button), WP-D (1024 date, phone copy, six-viewport sweep) |\n| Audit 4 Theming | 3 | 5 | WP-E (`!important` to zero in the observatory block), WP-F (dark toggle honoured) |\n| Audit 5 Anti-patterns | 3 | 5 | WP-A (one photograph, no glass on Today), WP-G (label count, text-shadow reliance) |\n| Critique 2 Real-world language | 3 | 5 | WP-H (copy pass) |\n| Critique 3 Control and freedom | 3 | 5 | WP-I (Escape, back, section memory) |\n| Critique 4 Consistency | 3 | 5 | WP-J (type scale tokens, one icon size, one radius set) |\n| Critique 5 Error prevention | 3 | 5 | WP-K (jump row validation, disabled-state reasons) |\n| Critique 7 Flexibility | 3 | 5 | WP-L (shortcut hints, resume last drill) |\n| Critique 8 Aesthetic and minimal | 3 | 5 | WP-A, WP-G, WP-D |\n| Critique 9 Error recovery | 3 | 5 | WP-M (every fetch has a retry and a plain sentence) |\n| Critique 10 Help | 2 | 5 | WP-N (inline \"what this is\" disclosure, first-run line, help link) |\n\nOrder of execution: A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, I, J, K, L, M, N. Push and publish after A+B, after C+D, after E+F, after G+H+I+J, after K+L+M+N. That is five publishes. Measure after each publish and record.\n\n---\n\n## Part 3. Work packages\n\nEach WP has: Why, Steps, Done when. Do the steps in order.\n\n### WP-A. One photograph, glass on one surface only\n\n**Why.** Four `backdrop-filter` surfaces cost paint time on phones (Performance) and the \"glass card over photo\" look is the single strongest tell of the aesthetic family (Anti-patterns, Aesthetic). The hero keeps its photograph; the Today card becomes flat near-black like the practice card. Glass stays only on the hero console, which sits over the photo.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. In classroom.css find the rule that puts the eclipse photograph behind Today. Search for `drill-observatory-today` near `background-image` or `url(`. Note the selector.\n2. Remove the photograph and the scrim `::before` from Today. Give Today the same surface as the practice card: `background: var(--observatory-surface-card)`.\n3. `.drill-observatory-today-primary` (line ~1821): delete the two `backdrop-filter` lines. Change `background` to `rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.04)` (a faint lift on flat black), keep the ring.\n4. `.drill-observatory-today-rail` (line ~1867): delete the two `backdrop-filter` lines. Background `rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.035)`. Hover: background `rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.06)`, no 24px shadow, keep the 2px lift.\n5. `.drill-observatory-today-quiet` (line ~1888): delete the two `backdrop-filter` lines. Same background as the rail.\n6. `.drill-observatory-console` (line ~1383): keep the blur. Reduce to `blur(12px)`.\n7. Text inside Today no longer sits on a photo, so remove every `text-shadow` inside Today (search `text-shadow` between the Today selectors). Text shadows stay only in the hero.\n8. Run 1e locally: `backdrop-filter` count must be 2 lines (one surface, plus its `-webkit-` twin).\n9. Re-run contrast (1a) after publish. Any Today text now measured against `#08080b` plus the faint lift must still pass 4.5. If a label fails, raise its ink step (never below ink-50).\n10. Update the surface brief: in \"The ground\", change \"Two of the three cards carry a photograph\" to \"One card, the hero, carries a photograph\"; in \"Depth\", change the glass sentence to \"Glass appears only on the hero console.\"\n\n**Done when.** One photo on the page, `backdrop-filter` appears on one selector, contrast scan 0 fails at 375 and 1280, tests pass, brief updated.\n\n### WP-B. Photo, fonts, and a Lighthouse number\n\n**Why.** Performance 5 needs evidence, not a feeling. Get a Lighthouse mobile performance score on the hub and fix whatever it names.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Find the hero photograph asset: search classroom.css for `url(` inside the `.drill-observatory-hero` rule (line ~1034). Note the file and its size on disk (`ls -la` the path under `public/` or `src/assets/`).\n2. If the file is over 250 KB or is a PNG/JPEG without a modern alternative: create a WebP at the display size (the hero is at most 1200px wide; export at 1600px for retina, quality 80) with `npx sharp-cli` or `cwebp`. If neither tool installs, STOP AND ASK. Reference the WebP with a fallback: use `image-set()` in the CSS background, or switch the hero to an `<img>` with `<picture>` if that is simpler. Keep the crop identical.\n3. Fonts: find where Fraunces, JetBrains Mono, and Outfit are loaded (search `index.html` and `src/index.css` for `fonts.googleapis` or `@font-face`). Ensure `display=swap` (Google) or `font-display: swap` (self-hosted). Add `<link rel=\"preconnect\">` for the font host in `index.html` if missing.\n4. Check the hub does not animate a layout property: search the observatory block for `transition:` lines that include `height`, `width`, `padding`, `margin`, `top`, `left`. Change each to `transform` or `opacity`, or remove the transition.\n5. Run Lighthouse on production in mobile mode. If the Chrome DevTools MCP is available, use `lighthouse_audit` on `https://lsatprep.study/classroom/drill`. If it is not, install and run it locally against production:\n```bash\nnpx lighthouse https://lsatprep.study/classroom/drill --preset=perf --form-factor=mobile --only-categories=performance --output=json --output-path=./lighthouse-drill.json --chrome-flags=\"--headless\"\n```\n   Note: the page needs a signed-in session, so an unauthenticated Lighthouse run may land on the sign-in page. If the report's `finalUrl` is not `/classroom/drill`, say so in the report and use the DevTools MCP route instead, or STOP AND ASK.\n6. Record the performance score, LCP, CLS, TBT. Fix anything Lighthouse names that is inside the hub (not the shell). Target: performance 90+, CLS under 0.05.\n7. Delete `lighthouse-drill.json` before committing.\n\n**Done when.** A recorded Lighthouse mobile score for the hub, WebP hero (or a note that the asset was already small), fonts swap, no layout-property transitions in the block.\n\n### WP-C. The phone menu button stops floating over content\n\n**Why.** On phones the menu button is `position: fixed` at 16px/16px, so once the student scrolls it sits on top of \"Your practice\" and the Today card. This is the one Responsive defect that is a real usability problem.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. In `ClassroomLayout.tsx` around line 1019 the button is rendered as a direct child of the root. Wrap it in a bar: `<div className=\"classroom-mobile-bar\">` containing the button and, to its right, a small text label with the section name (there is an `activeSection` value already in scope; render its display label, or \"Drill\" on the drill root). Keep the button's `aria-label`.\n2. In classroom.css, add:\n```css\n.classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar { display: none; }\n@media (max-width: 1023px) {\n  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-bar {\n    align-items: center;\n    background: var(--classroom-page);\n    border-bottom: 1px solid var(--classroom-line);\n    display: flex;\n    gap: 12px;\n    height: 56px;\n    padding: 0 12px;\n    position: sticky;\n    top: 0;\n    z-index: 70;\n  }\n  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-mobile-bar { background: var(--classroom-drill-page); }\n  .classroom-root .classroom-mobile-menu-button { position: static; }\n  .classroom-root .classroom-column { padding-top: 16px; }\n  .classroom-root.classroom-root-drill .classroom-stage { padding-top: var(--observatory-space-4); }\n}\n```\n   Check the existing rule at classroom.css ~3114 (`padding: 72px 0 90px`) and the drill stage rules at ~1984 and ~2014 (`padding-top: 72px`). Change those 72px values to 16px, because the bar now takes its own space. Do the same for `.classroom-root-schedule` at ~3119.\n3. The bar is sticky, so it stays at the top while scrolling but is part of the flow. Confirm the jump row starts right under it with no overlap at 375.\n4. Every other classroom section (Home, Messages, Schedule, Workspace) shares this shell. Open each on production at 375 after publish and screenshot the top. Nothing may overlap the bar. If a section had its own 72px allowance somewhere else, adjust it.\n5. The bar text: mono label style is wrong here (this is the shell, not the hub). Use the shell's own label style, `--classroom-meta-text`, 13px, weight 500.\n\n**Done when.** At 375 and 768, scrolled to the bottom of the hub, the menu button is in a bar above the content and covers nothing. All five classroom sections screenshot clean at 375.\n\n### WP-D. 1024px date, phone console copy, six-viewport sweep\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Date at 1024: in `AcademyMainhub.tsx` line ~90, compute a short label too: `monthShort` with `{ month: \"short\", day: \"numeric\" }`. Render both spans: `<span className=\"drill-observatory-date-long\">` and `<span className=\"drill-observatory-date-short\">`. In classroom.css: `.drill-observatory-date-short { display: none; }` and inside `@media (min-width: 900px) and (max-width: 1099px) { .drill-observatory-date-long { display: none } .drill-observatory-date-short { display: inline } }`. Result at 1024: \"TUESDAY · SEP 8 · 2026\" on one line.\n2. Console copy on phones (the paragraph at AcademyMainhub.tsx ~line 213 runs five lines at 375). Replace the LR sentence with: \"Pick PrepTests, question types, and difficulty. See the exact set before you start.\" Replace the RC sentence with: \"Pick PrepTests, topics, and difficulty. Choose the passages or let the set fill itself.\" These are shorter on every viewport, so no phone-only variant is needed.\n3. The fallback hero paragraph (no assignment, line ~161) is fine at 375; leave it.\n4. Sweep: after publish, run Part 1 at all six widths. Fix anything that wraps badly, overlaps, or overflows. Typical culprits: the Next up facts line at 375 when the source name is long (let it wrap, it is `flex-wrap`), the section switch beside the search at 768.\n5. Add a test in `AcademyMainhub.test.tsx` that the short and long date spans both render (query by text matching `/\\d{4}$/`).\n\n**Done when.** Six viewports measured clean, date one line at 1024, console paragraph at most three lines at 375.\n\n### WP-E. `!important` to zero inside the observatory block\n\n**Why.** 200 `!important` declarations exist to beat shadcn's Card and Button styles. Theming 5 needs the cascade to work without force.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Understand why they are there: `Card` and `Button` from `src/components/ui/` apply Tailwind utility classes (`rounded-lg border bg-card shadow-sm`, `inline-flex h-10 px-4 ...`). Tailwind utilities have single-class specificity; the observatory rules are also mostly single-class, so the author used `!important` to win. The fix is specificity, not force.\n2. Wrap every observatory selector in the block (lines ~840 to ~2060) with the root: `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-cta { ... }` has (0,2,0) and beats any utility. Do this mechanically with a script, not by hand. Write `scripts/observatory-specificity.mjs` that:\n   - reads classroom.css,\n   - for every rule between the line containing `.drill-observatory-root {` and the line containing `/* end observatory */` (add that comment marker at the end of the block first, after the reduced-motion rule at ~2055),\n   - for each selector in a rule's selector list that starts with `.drill-observatory-` and is not already prefixed, prefixes `.drill-observatory-root `,\n   - strips ` !important` from every declaration in that range,\n   - writes the file back and prints counts.\n   Media queries inside the range are handled the same way (the script only touches selector lines and declaration lines; braces stay).\n3. Run the script once. Run typecheck and tests. Publish (with WP-F). Then measure: screenshot at 375 and 1280 and compare to the WP-D screenshots side by side. Anything that changed shape means a rule lost to a utility. Find the rule, raise its specificity by one more class (for example `.drill-observatory-root .drill-observatory-hero .drill-observatory-cta`), never by `!important`.\n4. Known hot spots to check first after the script: the CTA (`h-10` and `px-4` from Button), the console start button, the hero Card (`rounded-lg border shadow-sm`), the practice Card, Today's inner Card, the `min-height` on the hero grid.\n5. The rules for `.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill ...` (lines ~840 to ~980) are outside the observatory root; leave their `!important`s for this WP but count them separately in the report.\n6. Target: `grep -c '!important'` on the file drops from 200 to under 30, and zero of the remaining are inside the observatory block. Record before and after counts.\n7. Update the surface brief \"Known debt\": remove the `!important` line and replace it with one sentence: \"Observatory rules are scoped under `.drill-observatory-root` and win by specificity, not `!important`.\"\n\n**Done when.** Zero `!important` between the observatory markers, page looks identical at 375 and 1280 to the WP-D screenshots, tests pass.\n\n### WP-F. The hub honours the theme toggle\n\n**Why.** The app has a light/dark toggle (`ThemeContext.tsx` adds `.light` to `<html>`; default is dark). The hub renders the same cream stage in both. Theming 5 means the room responds.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Find where the hub's stage tokens are declared: `--observatory-stage-well`, `--observatory-stage-ink`, `--observatory-stage-muted`, `--observatory-stage-hair`, `--observatory-stage-lit` (classroom.css ~line 862 onward, under `.classroom-root.classroom-root-drill, .drill-observatory-root`). Also `--classroom-drill-page` at lines 22 (light, `#fdf8f3`) and 59 (dark, `#121019`).\n2. Decide the dark stage: `--classroom-drill-page: #121019` already exists for dark. Add a dark set of stage tokens next to it in the same dark block: `--observatory-stage-well: #1a1822; --observatory-stage-ink: #ece8e1; --observatory-stage-muted: #a39e98; --observatory-stage-hair: rgba(236,232,225,0.12); --observatory-stage-lit: #211f2a`. The cards stay `#08080b` in both themes (they are already dark).\n3. Make the observatory block read those tokens from the theme rather than redeclaring them. If the observatory root redeclares `--observatory-stage-*` with literal values, change those lines to `var(--classroom-stage-well)` style aliases, or move the declarations up into the light and dark blocks at lines ~22 and ~59.\n4. The jump row and section switch use `--observatory-stage-*` (via `--qj-*` aliases in questionJump.css and the toggle rules). Confirm they follow.\n5. Contrast in dark: stage muted `#a39e98` on `#1a1822` is about 6.2:1, ink is about 12:1. Verify with 1a after publish, in both themes. Toggle the theme from the account menu in the browser; if you cannot find the toggle, ask Joshua where it is.\n6. Update the surface brief \"The ground\": \"The stage is cream in light theme and near-black `#121019` in dark; the cards are `#08080b` in both.\" Remove the dark-toggle line from Known debt.\n\n**Done when.** Toggling the theme changes the stage, the jump row, and the section switch; contrast passes in both; the brief says so.\n\n### WP-G. Fewer labels, less text-shadow, quieter hero\n\n**Why.** Anti-patterns and Aesthetic. The mono uppercase micro-label is used many times on one screen; each one is a decoration when it is not carrying a fact. Text-shadow on every hero line is doing the scrim's job.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Count `drill-observatory-label` usages rendered on the default LR stage (search the three TSX files). List them in the report. Keep: the \"Next up\" facts line, the date, the \"Your practice\" and \"Also today\" card headings, the insight kicker. Remove: any label that repeats the heading above it, and any label on a control (a button says what it does in sentence case).\n2. Hero text-shadows: with the scrim doing its job, reduce `text-shadow` on `.drill-observatory-greeting h1` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.35)` and on `.drill-observatory-greeting p`, `.drill-observatory-next-title`, `.drill-observatory-quiet-link` to `0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`. Then re-run 1a and check by eye at the lightest crop of the photo. If any hero text is hard to read, darken the scrim by 0.05 alpha rather than restoring the shadow.\n3. The quiet link underline: `text-decoration-color: rgba(244, 241, 234, 0.55)` (was 0.35).\n4. The CTA's `box-shadow: 0 8px 32px rgba(0,0,0,0.4)` (classroom.css ~1249): reduce to `0 6px 20px rgba(0,0,0,0.3)`.\n5. Screenshot the hero at 1280 before and after; put both in the report.\n\n**Done when.** Label count on the LR default stage is at most 6, shadows reduced, contrast still 0 fails.\n\n### WP-H. Copy pass in the teaching voice\n\n**Why.** Critique 2 is about the student's words, not the product's. Read `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` first.\n\n**Steps.**\n1. List every string a student sees on the hub (hero, console, practice, Today, jump row, search trigger, section switch, loading and error states). Put the list in the report with a column for the new text.\n2. Rules for the new text: sentence case on buttons and headings; the verb first on buttons; say what the thing does before naming it; no \"Smart\" as a bare adjective without the sentence explaining it; no \"instead\" links that assume the student rejected the first option.\n3. Specific changes:\n   - Quiet link \"Start a Smart Drill instead\" becomes \"Or practice your weakest types\". RC: \"Or read one passage now\".\n   - Fallback CTA \"Start Smart Drill\" becomes \"Practice your weakest types\". RC \"Start One Passage\" becomes \"Read one passage\".\n   - Fallback paragraph: \"Start with the question types you miss most, or build a drill around exactly what you want to practice.\" RC: \"Read one passage now, untimed, with the answers after it. Or build a set of passages around what you want to read.\"\n   - \"Set up an LR drill\" stays. \"Set up an RC drill\" stays.\n   - \"Analytics\" and \"Journal\" links stay.\n   - Insight action labels: check each in `insightSlides.ts` against rule 2. Rewrite any that names a screen instead of an outcome (\"Open analytics\" becomes \"See where you lose points\").\n   - Today \"Also today\" stays. Its empty line stays.\n   - Jump row placeholder stays (\"PT101.S2.Q1\" form).\n4. The heading in the console \"Build the drill you need.\" stays; it is the one door and it says what it does.\n5. Update tests that assert the old strings (`AcademyMainhub.test.tsx` looks for \"Start Smart Drill\" and similar; search the three test files for each old string).\n6. Add the new strings to `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` under a dated heading so future work does not revert them.\n\n**Done when.** Every string in the list has been checked, tests updated and green, the corrections log has the entry.\n\n### WP-I. Control and freedom: Escape, back, section memory\n\n**Steps.**\n1. Escape closes a stage. In `AcademyMainhub.tsx`, where `stage` is read from the URL and `closeStage` exists (~line 560), add a `useEffect` that listens for `keydown` on `window`; if `event.key === \"Escape\"` and `stage !== \"default\"` and the event target is not inside an open dialog or a text input with content, call `closeStage()`. Clean up the listener.\n2. Browser back returns to the hub. Check that opening a stage uses `navigate` with a new history entry (not `replace: true`), so back works. If it uses replace, change it to push. Test by opening the builder on production, pressing the browser back button, and confirming the hub returns with the same section.\n3. Section memory. When the student switches LR/RC, persist the choice in `localStorage` under `lsatu.drillHub.section`. On mount, if the URL has no `section` param, read it and apply it. Wrap the storage calls in `try/catch`.\n4. Cancel in the builder keeps the student's choices for the session: if `QuestionPicker` already holds its state in a store or URL, leave it; if it resets on Cancel, note that in the report (do not build persistence for the builder in this plan).\n5. Contrast scan\n\n```js\n(() => {\n  const L = ([r,g,b]) => { const f = c => { c /= 255; return c <= 0.03928 ? c/12.92 : ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4; }; return 0.2126*f(r)+0.7152*f(g)+0.0722*f(b); };\n  const parse = s => { const m = (s.match(/[\\d.]+/g)||[]).map(Number); return { rgb: m.slice(0,3), a: m.length > 3 ? m[3] : 1 }; };\n  const over = (top, a, under) => top.map((c,i) => Math.round(c*a + under[i]*(1-a)));\n  const bgOf = el => { let n = el, stack = []; while (n && n.nodeType === 1) { const { rgb, a } = parse(getComputedStyle(n).backgroundColor); if (a > 0) stack.push({ rgb, a }); if (a >= 1) break; n = n.parentElement; } let out = [255,255,255]; for (let i = stack.length-1; i >= 0; i--) out = over(stack[i].rgb, stack[i].a, out); return out; };\n  const ratio = (a, b) => { const x = L(a), y = L(b); return (Math.max(x,y)+0.05)/(Math.min(x,y)+0.05); };\n  const root = document.querySelector('.drill-observatory-root') || document.body;\n  const walker = document.createTreeWalker(root, NodeFilter.SHOW_TEXT);\n  const fails = [], seen = new Set(); let count = 0, node;\n  while ((node = walker.nextNode())) {\n    const t = node.textContent.trim(); if (!t) continue;\n    const el = node.parentElement; if (seen.has(el)) continue; seen.add(el);\n    const cs = getComputedStyle(el); if (cs.visibility === 'hidden' || cs.display === 'none') continue;\n    const r = el.getBoundingClientRect(); if (r.width === 0 || r.height === 0) continue;\n    const fg = parse(cs.color); const fgRgb = fg.a < 1 ? over(fg.rgb, fg.a, bgOf(el)) : fg.rgb;\n    const bg = bgOf(el); const c = ratio(fgRgb, bg);\n    const size = parseFloat(cs.fontSize), bold = parseInt(cs.fontWeight) >= 700;\n    const large = size >= 24 || (size >= 18.66 && bold);\n    const need = large ? 3 : 4.5; count++;\n    if (c < need) fails.push({ text: t.slice(0,40), ratio: +c.toFixed(2), need, color: cs.color, bg: `rgb(${bg})`, cls: el.className.toString().slice(0,60) });\n  }\n  return { checked: count, fails };\n})()\n```\n\nCaveat: text over a photograph reports the scrim colour, not the photo. For the hero (and Today, until WP-A removes its photo), also zoom the screenshot and confirm by eye that the lightest part of the photo under any text still reads.
